1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Synthesis.}
5	VLA fine-tuning is associated with two representational changes (peak-to-exit action-probe decodability drops and counterfactual sensitivity) that concentrate at the readout token (training shifts readout $R^2$ by $+0.069$ vs.\ image\_mean by $+0.015$).
6	L1 instruction attention is inherited from base-model pretraining (Phi-3-Vision: 32/32, Qwen2.5-VL: 13--14/16 before training).
7	VLA fine-tuning does not build instruction routing from scratch; downstream representations differ between trained and reference models while early-layer attention structure is preserved.
8	
9	\paragraph{VLM-to-VLA vs.\ VLA-to-VLA transitions.}
10	Exit collapse separates cleanly by effect size: substantial ($\Delta R^2 = 0.156$--$0.455$, all $> 0.10$) in every VLM-to-VLA trained cell, but only small fluctuations ($\Delta R^2 = 0.033$--$0.086$, all $< 0.10$) under VLA-to-VLA conditions.
11	Two independent VLM-to-VLA transitions (TracVLA and NORA, with distinct backbone families) converge on the same large effect, compatible with task-structure constraints of causal-decoder VLA design rather than architecture-specific artifacts.
12	The small $\Delta R^2$ under VLA-to-VLA conditions is consistent with the OXE-pretrained baseline having already adapted at exit layers during its initial action training on 970K episodes; LIBERO fine-tuning, operating on a model that has already adapted for action prediction, does not produce further substantial collapse.
13	Mid-layer probes outperform exit-layer probes for action prediction in architectures exhibiting pronounced collapse, suggesting potential efficiency gains from early-exit inference.
14	Correlating trained $\Delta R^2$ with LIBERO success rates yields a positive but non-significant trend ($\rho = 0.583$, $p = 0.10$, $n = 9$; Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_discussion_appendix}).
15

_source line 10833 · 2026-05-23T17:22:35.958067+00:00_

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	Vision-language-action models (VLAs) fine-tune pretrained language models on robot demonstrations to produce actions from visual observations and language instructions~\cite{brohan2023_rt2,kim2024_openvla}.
5	In these decoder-only architectures, image tokens precede instruction tokens, and the causal attention mask prevents image tokens from attending to later tokens~\cite{wang2025seeing,pei2025rethinking}.
6	The readout token (the final position before action decoding) is the sole multimodal integration point.
7	How representations at this position differ between trained and reference models, and whether the resulting patterns reflect training or inherited backbone structure, remains unknown.
8	
9	Behavioral studies show that VLAs exhibit visual dominance~\cite{grant2026} and underweight instructions~\cite{haon2025}, but existing representational analyses target single architectures.
10	No prior work tests whether instruction routing mechanisms are consistent across backbone families or decomposes training-associated effects from inherited base-model priors.
11	
12	We address this gap with a cross-architecture analysis of three VLAs (OpenVLA~\cite{kim2024_openvla}, TracVLA~\cite{zheng2024tracevla}, and NORA~\cite{hung2025nora}) across four LIBERO~\cite{liu2023_libero} task suites (\S\ref{sec:setup}).
13	TracVLA and NORA baselines are pure VLMs ($N = 2$ independent VLM-to-VLA transitions); OpenVLA's baseline is the OXE-pretrained~\cite{padalkar2023_oxe} VLA (VLA-to-VLA transition).
14

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
5	\label{sec:setup}
6	
7	We analyze three VLA architectures across four LIBERO~\cite{liu2023_libero} task suites (Goal, Spatial, Object, Long-Horizon) in a $3 \times 4$ cross-architecture matrix (Table~\ref{tab:cross_arch_matrix}).
8	The architectures span distinct backbone families: OpenVLA~\cite{kim2024_openvla,karamcheti2024} (Llama-2-7B~\cite{touvron2023_llama2}, 32 layers, 32 MHA heads), TracVLA~\cite{zheng2024tracevla} (Phi-3-mini~\cite{abdin2024phi3}, 32 layers, 32 heads), and NORA~\cite{hung2025nora} (Qwen2.5-VL-3B, 36 layers, GQA with 16 query heads and 2 KV heads).
9	Of the 12 matrix cells, 10 are filled; NORA Object and Long-Horizon lack available checkpoints.
10	Each architecture is compared in trained and reference conditions, with a critical asymmetry between reference baselines: the OpenVLA reference checkpoint is the OXE-pretrained~\cite{padalkar2023_oxe} VLA (already fine-tuned for action prediction on 970K episodes), while the TracVLA and NORA reference checkpoints are pure vision-language models (Phi-3-Vision and Qwen2.5-VL) with no action training.
11	Three analyses are applied to each cell (\S\ref{sec:method}): $R^2$ action probes at all layers, L1 attention head profiling for instruction dominance, and counterfactual instruction perturbation.
12	For OpenVLA LIBERO-Goal, $N = 63{,}228$ observations; other cells are at comparable scale.
13	For trained models that produce action outputs, counterfactual sensitivity is measured via Cohen's $d$ on action MSE.
14	For reference VLMs (Phi-3-Vision, Qwen2.5-VL) that do not produce actions, sensitivity is measured via cosine distance on hidden representations; these metrics operate on different scales and are not directly comparable (\S\ref{sec:cf_sensitivity}).
15	OpenVLA's OXE-pretrained checkpoint produces actions, permitting same-metric (Cohen's $d$) comparison in that condition.
16	Bonferroni correction details app
…[truncated 219 chars]

```json
  "old_string": "\\caption{L1 instruction-dominant head counts across three architectures in trained and reference conditions.\nL1 instruction attention is a base-model property: the TracVLA reference (Phi-3-Vision) saturates at 32/32 heads before any VLA training, and training \\emph{reduces} this to 27--30/32.\nNORA (Qwen2.5-VL, 16-head GQA) shows 13--14/16 in both conditions, unchanged by training.\nOpenVLA ranges from 14--24/32 trained vs.\\ 24--26/32 reference (OXE-pretrained).\nVLA fine-tuning does not create L1 instruction routing; it inherits and occasionally diminishes what the pretrained backbone provides.}",
  "new_string": "\\caption{L1 instruction-dominant head counts across three architectures in trained and reference conditions.\nTracVLA reference (Phi-3-Vision): 32/32; trained: 27--30/32.\nNORA reference (Qwen2.5-VL, 16-head GQA): 13--14/16; trained: 12--13/16.\nOpenVLA reference (OXE-pretrained): 24--26/32; trained: 14--24/32.}"
}
```

```json
}
```

_source line 10858 · 2026-05-23T17:40:11.065416+00:00_

30	VLM-to-VLA fine-tuning is associated with substantial exit collapse across all tested conditions: TracVLA~\cite{zheng2024tracevla} $\Delta R^2 = 0.156$--$0.362$ across all four suites, and NORA~\cite{hung2025nora} $\Delta R^2 = 0.308$ (Spatial) and $0.455$ (Goal) (Figure~\ref{fig:r2_collapse}), all exceeding the $\Delta R^2 > 0.10$ threshold for substantive collapse (\S\ref{sec:cross_arch_analyses}).
31	Under VLA-to-VLA conditions, OpenVLA~\cite{kim2024_openvla} shows only small fluctuations across all four suites ($\Delta R^2 = 0.033$--$0.086$, all below $0.10$); reference baselines similarly show $\Delta R^2 \leq 0.097$.
32	The magnitude gap is consistent: the smallest VLM-to-VLA trained $\Delta R^2$ ($0.156$, TracVLA Spatial) exceeds the largest VLA-to-VLA trained $\Delta R^2$ ($0.086$, OpenVLA Goal) by a factor of ${\sim}2\times$.
33	Monte Carlo cross-validation supports this separation: VLM-to-VLA trained CIs lie entirely above zero (lower bounds $\geq 0.09$), while VLA-to-VLA trained CIs span or nearly span zero (Table~\ref{tab:cross_arch_matrix}).
34	
35	\paragraph{NLP finetuning control.}
36	To test whether exit collapse is specific to action-oriented training or a generic fine-tuning artifact, we probe Llama-2-7b-chat (the same Llama-2-7B backbone as OpenVLA), fine-tuned via supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF) for conversational use, without an action head or any robot demonstration data.
37	This control isolates the effect of action-oriented training: the backbone architecture, model scale (7B parameters), and fine-tuning regime (instruction tuning on the base Llama-2-7B) are matched, while the training objective (chat vs.\ action prediction) differs.
38	Across all 32 layers, the $R^2$ action-probe profile is completely flat ($R^2 = 0.155 \pm 0.001$, $\Delta R^2 = 0.000$), showing no peak-to-exit drop.
39	By comparison, VLM-to-VLA trained models exhibit substantial collapse ($\Delta R^2 = 0.156$--$0.455$);

111	\subsection{Instruction Sensitivity: Counterfactual Perturbation}
112	\label{sec:cf_sensitivity}
113	
114	All trained VLAs exhibit medium-to-large counterfactual sensitivity to instruction perturbation.
115	Across eight cells where Cohen's $d$ is applicable (OpenVLA and TracVLA, four suites each), every perturbation condition produces $d = 0.45$--$1.97$ (Table~\ref{tab:counterfactual} in Appendix~\ref{sec:cf_details_appendix}).
116	The ``empty'' condition ranks first in 7 of 8 cells ($d = 0.98$--$1.52$); ``paraphrased'' ranks last in 7 of 8, indicating that VLAs encode instruction content beyond token-level matching.
117	TracVLA produces systematically higher effect sizes ($d = 0.97$--$1.52$) than OpenVLA ($0.45$--$1.21$).
118	NORA uses cosine distance (saturated at $1.0$ on Goal; peak $0.275$ on Spatial).
119	Metric incomparability between trained (Cohen's $d$) and reference (cosine distance) conditions precludes cross-metric amplification ratios (see Appendix~\ref{sec:cf_details_appendix} for metric discussion).
120	
121	Two OpenVLA cells permit same-metric trained/reference comparison, since the OXE-pretrained baseline also produces actions.
122	On Spatial, training increases sensitivity across all four conditions ($+0.05$ to $+0.24$), consistent with the small $\Delta R^2 = 0.037$ (below the $0.10$ threshold).

_source line 10874 · 2026-05-23T17:42:12.030615+00:00_

130	The TracVLA reference (Phi-3-Vision) allocates 32/32 heads to instruction-dominant attention across all four suites, saturating the capacity of its 32-head L1 before any VLA training.
131	Training slightly \emph{reduces} this count to 27--30/32, with freed heads presumably repurposed for action-related computation.
132	The NORA reference (Qwen2.5-VL) allocates 13--14 of its 16 L1 heads to instruction dominance (81--88\%), and training leaves this essentially unchanged at 12--13/16.
133	OpenVLA shows 24--26/32 instruction-dominant heads in the OXE-pretrained baseline; trained values are similar (14--24/32), with the largest change in Long-Horizon where training reduces the count from 25 to 14 heads (Figure~\ref{fig:cross_arch_l1}).
134	
135	If VLA training drove L1 instruction routing, reference VLMs should show low attention that increases after training, as observed in OpenVLA's random-weight condition (0/32; \S\ref{sec:openvla_routing}).
136	Instead, both reference VLMs already saturate or near-saturate, and training leaves these counts unchanged or slightly reduced.
137	L1 instruction attention is an inherited base-model prior, not a product of VLA fine-tuning.
138	
139	\begin{figure}[t]
140	\centering
141	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_cross_arch_l1.pdf}
142	\caption{L1 instruction-dominant head counts across three architectures in trained and reference conditions.
143	TracVLA reference (Phi-3-Vision): 32/32; trained: 27--30/32.
144	NORA reference (Qwen2.5-VL, 16-head GQA): 13--14/16; trained: 12--13/16.
145	OpenVLA reference (OXE-pretrained): 24--26/32; trained: 14--24/32.}
146	\label{fig:cross_arch_l1}
147	\end{figure}

83	\paragraph{$R^2$ localization.}
84	$R^2$ action probing localizes training effects: trained readout peaks at $R^2 = 0.678$ (L13), reference readout peaks at $0.609$ (L32), and the training effect concentrates at the readout ($+0.069$) rather than at image\_mean ($+0.015$; \S\ref{sec:action_r2_threecond}).
85	These effects survive Bonferroni correction across all 63 tests (\S\ref{sec:bonferroni_correction}).

113	
114	\paragraph{Metric constraints on trained--reference comparison.}
115	Trained models produce action outputs, enabling Cohen's $d$ on action MSE (OpenVLA, TracVLA) or cosine distance on action tokens (NORA).
116	Reference VLMs (Phi-3-Vision, Qwen2.5-VL) do not generate actions; their sensitivity is measured via cosine distance on hidden representations---a metric with different scale, units, and distributional properties.
117	Ratios between Cohen's $d$ and cosine distance are therefore methodologically invalid, and we do not report cross-metric amplification factors.
118	Qualitatively, reference VLMs show weak sensitivity: the TracVLA reference (Phi-3-Vision) produces peak cosine distances of $0.15$--$0.19$; the NORA reference (Qwen2.5-VL) yields $0.081$ on Goal.
119	
120	\paragraph{NORA details.}

_source line 10890 · 2026-05-23T17:43:05.890957+00:00_

120	
121	Two OpenVLA cells permit same-metric trained/reference comparison, since the OXE-pretrained baseline also produces actions.
122	On Spatial, training increases sensitivity across all four conditions ($+0.05$ to $+0.24$), consistent with the small $\Delta R^2 = 0.037$ (below the $0.10$ threshold).
123	On Long-Horizon, the pattern inverts: the OXE-pretrained model ($d = 1.05$) exceeds the LIBERO-trained model ($d = 0.56$), consistent with small collapse ($\Delta R^2 = 0.033$) and the general-purpose model maintaining broader instruction responsiveness.
124	
125	%------------------------------------------------------------------
126	\subsection{Inherited Routing: L1 Attention Profiling}
127	\label{sec:l1_priors}
128	
129	L1 instruction-dominant head counts across architectures reveal a pattern that contrasts with the training-associated effects documented in \S\ref{sec:r2_collapse} and \S\ref{sec:cf_sensitivity}.
130	The TracVLA reference (Phi-3-Vision) allocates 32/32 heads to instruction-dominant attention across all four suites, saturating the capacity of its 32-head L1 before any VLA training.
131	Training slightly \emph{reduces} this count to 27--30/32, with freed heads presumably repurposed for action-related computation.
132	The NORA reference (Qwen2.5-VL) allocates 13--14 of its 16 L1 heads to instruction dominance (81--88\%), and training leaves this essentially unchanged at 12--13/16.
133	OpenVLA shows 24--26/32 instruction-dominant heads in the OXE-pretrained baseline; trained values are similar (14--24/32), with the largest change in Long-Horizon where training reduces the count from 25 to 14 heads (Figure~\ref{fig:cross_arch_l1}).
134

_source line 10906 · 2026-05-23T17:46:47.186964+00:00_

113	
114	\paragraph{Metric constraints on trained--reference comparison.}
115	Trained models produce action outputs, enabling Cohen's $d$ on action MSE (OpenVLA, TracVLA) or cosine distance on action tokens (NORA).
116	Reference VLMs (Phi-3-Vision, Qwen2.5-VL) do not generate actions; their sensitivity is measured via cosine distance on hidden representations---a metric with different scale, units, and distributional properties.
117	Ratios between Cohen's $d$ and cosine distance are therefore methodologically invalid, and we do not report cross-metric amplification factors.
118	Qualitatively, reference VLMs show weak sensitivity: the TracVLA reference (Phi-3-Vision) produces peak cosine distances of $0.15$--$0.19$; the NORA reference (Qwen2.5-VL) yields $0.081$ on Goal.

_source line 10910 · 2026-05-23T17:46:48.643748+00:00_

1002	
1003	\paragraph{Convergent reorganization from divergent starting points.}
1004	Untrained profiles differ qualitatively: OpenVLA (Llama-2) peaks at L0 ($R^2 = 0.629$) then collapses to near-zero at mid-layers (L20: $0.086$, std $= 0.311$), while Phi-3-V peaks at L6 ($0.578 \pm 0.018$) with gradual monotonic decline (late-layer std $= 0.068$).
1005	After VLA training, both architectures reach nearly identical mid-layer peaks---OpenVLA at L9 ($R^2 = 0.637 \pm 0.008$), TraceVLA at L11 ($0.634 \pm 0.009$)---with $\Delta = 0.003$ and overlapping CIs.
1006	Deep-layer variance drops sharply with training: OpenVLA std $0.277 \to 0.040$; Phi-3-V std $0.068 \to 0.011$.
1007	Peak $R^2$ values are comparable before and after training (reference peaks reach $91$--$99\%$ of trained), but peak locations shift deeper (L0$\to$L9; L6$\to$L11) and profile shapes transform from architecture-specific patterns to a shared mid-layer plateau.

### Tool result

_source line 10912 · 2026-05-23T17:46:48.895753+00:00_

1118	Per-layer profiles suggest three stages: redistribution (L0--L1; probe $99.6\%$, 24/32 instruction-dominant heads), computation (L8--L13; $R^2$ peak $0.655$--$0.678$), and decay (L14--L31, ${\sim}56\%$ of parameters; declining $R^2$ and RSA suggest pruning potential).
1119	Task-specific fine-tuning eliminates L31 re-engagement (18 $\to$ 0 heads; \S\ref{sec:openvla_routing}), consistent with exit-layer attention serving multi-task flexibility ($N{=}2$).
1120	TraceVLA~\cite{zheng2024tracevla} (Phi-3-V) shows consistent entry-exit attention concentration from an opposite starting point (\S\ref{sec:openvla_routing}).
1121	The $2 {\times} 2$ trained-vs-reference comparison reveals a consistent reorganization pattern: reference OpenVLA collapses from L0 ($R^2 = 0.629$) to near-zero at mid-layers (L20: $0.086$, std $= 0.277$), while reference Phi-3-V peaks at L6 ($0.578$) with gradual decline (std $= 0.068$)---qualitatively opposite reference landscapes.
1122	Yet training drives both to nearly identical mid-layer peaks ($0.637$ vs.\ $0.634$, $\Delta = 0.003$) with stabilized deep-layer variance (OpenVLA std $0.277 \to 0.040$; Phi-3-V $0.068 \to 0.011$).
1123	This consistent outcome from divergent starting points suggests that mid-layer action specialization is a consistent convergence pattern of VLA training, not an architectural artifact.

0	\section{Experiments}
1	\label{sec:experiments}
2	
3	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
4	\label{sec:setup}
5	
6	We apply all three analyses (\S\ref{sec:cross_arch_analyses}) to the $3 \times 4$ architecture-suite matrix described in \S\ref{sec:arch_data} (Table~\ref{tab:cross_arch_matrix}).
7	Of the 12 cells, 10 are filled; NORA Object and Long-Horizon lack available checkpoints.
8	For OpenVLA LIBERO-Goal, $N = 63{,}228$ observations; other cells are at comparable scale.
9	For trained models that produce action outputs, counterfactual sensitivity is measured via Cohen's $d$ on action MSE.
10	For reference VLMs (Phi-3-Vision, Qwen2.5-VL) that do not produce actions, sensitivity is measured via cosine distance on hidden representations; these metrics operate on different scales and are not directly comparable (\S\ref{sec:cf_sensitivity}).
11	OpenVLA's OXE-pretrained checkpoint produces actions, permitting same-metric (Cohen's $d$) comparison in that condition.
12	Bonferroni correction details appear in Appendix~\ref{sec:bonferroni_correction}.
13	
14	%------------------------------------------------------------------
15	\subsection{OpenVLA: Motivating Single-Architecture Analysis}
16	\label{sec:openvla_routing}
17	
18	Before testing cross-architectural generality, we establish three core phenomena on OpenVLA~\cite{kim2024_openvla} (full analysis in Appendix~\ref{sec:openvla_deepdive_appendix}).
19	At L1, three conditions form a gradient: random-weight initialization allocates 0/32 instruction-dominant heads, Llama-2 base~\cite{touvron2023_llama2} allocates 9/32, and VLA-trained allocates 24/32, supporting the view that VLA fine-tuning is associated with instruction routing beyond language-model priors.

110	%------------------------------------------------------------------
111	\subsection{Instruction Sensitivity: Counterfactual Perturbation}
112	\label{sec:cf_sensitivity}
113	
114	All trained VLAs exhibit medium-to-large counterfactual sensitivity to instruction perturbation (Table~\ref{tab:cf_sensitivity}).
115	Across eight cells where Cohen's $d$ is applicable (OpenVLA and TracVLA, four suites each), every perturbation condition yields $d = 0.45$--$1.97$ (Table~\ref{tab:counterfactual_main}).
116	The ``empty'' condition ranks first in 7 of 8 cells ($d = 0.98$--$1.52$); ``paraphrased'' ranks last in 7 of 8, indicating that VLAs encode instruction content beyond token-level matching.
117	TracVLA produces systematically higher effect sizes ($d = 0.97$--$1.52$) than OpenVLA ($0.45$--$1.21$).
118	NORA uses cosine distance (saturated at $1.0$ on Goal; peak $0.275$ on Spatial).
119	Metric incomparability between trained (Cohen's $d$) and reference (cosine distance) conditions precludes cross-metric amplification ratios (see Appendix~\ref{sec:cf_details_appendix} for metric discussion).
120	
121	Two OpenVLA cells permit same-metric trained/reference comparison, since the OXE-pretrained baseline also produces actions.
122	On Spatial, training increases sensitivity across all four conditions ($+0.05$ to $+0.24$), consistent with the small $\Delta R^2 = 0.037$ (below the $0.10$ threshold).

0	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
1	\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Scope of claims.}
4	Our analyses are observational: we compare trained and reference checkpoints but do not intervene on the training process itself.
5	All findings are correlational associations between training status and representational structure; we cannot distinguish whether exit collapse is caused by action fine-tuning or by confounded factors (e.g., data distribution, optimization trajectory).
6	The cross-architecture design ($N = 2$ independent VLM-to-VLA transitions) strengthens external validity relative to single-architecture studies but remains insufficient for strong causal claims.
7	We use ``associated with'' rather than ``produces'' throughout to reflect this evidential status.
8	
9	\paragraph{Synthesis.}
10	VLA fine-tuning is associated with two representational changes (peak-to-exit action-probe decodability drops and counterfactual sensitivity) that concentrate at the readout token (training shifts readout $R^2$ by $+0.069$ vs.\ image\_mean by $+0.015$).
11	L1 instruction attention is inherited from base-model pretraining (Phi-3-Vision: 32/32, Qwen2.5-VL: 13--14/16 before training).
12	VLA fine-tuning does not build instruction routing from scratch; downstream representations differ between trained and reference models while early-layer attention structure is preserved.
13	
14	\paragraph{VLM-to-VLA vs.\ VLA-to-VLA transitions.}
15	Exit collapse separates cleanly by effect size: substantial ($\Delta R^2 = 0.156$--$0.455$, all $> 0.10$) in every VLM-to-VLA trained cell, but only small fluctuations ($\Delta R^2 = 0.033$--$0.086$, all $< 0.10$) under VLA-to-VLA conditions.
16	Two independent VLM-to-VLA transitions (TracVLA and NORA, with distinct backbone families) converge on the same large effect, compatible with task-structure constraints of causal-decoder VLA design rather than architecture-specific artifacts.
17	The small $\Delta R^2$ under VLA-to-VLA conditions is con
…[truncated 2248 chars]

_source line 10986 · 2026-05-23T17:55:36.746721+00:00_

1128	\subsection{Extended Discussion}
1129	\label{sec:extended_discussion_appendix}
1130	
1131	\paragraph{Observational three-tier gradient.}
1132	The cross-architecture matrix suggests a three-tier gradient of representational adaptation.
1133	Tier~1 (VLM bases, no action training): inherited L1 attention (TracVLA 32/32, NORA 13--14/16), negligible exit collapse ($\Delta R^2 \leq 0.048$), and minimal counterfactual sensitivity (cosine distance $\leq 0.185$).
1134	Tier~2 (general-purpose VLA, OXE-pretrained): strong L1 routing (24--26/32 heads), moderate counterfactual sensitivity ($d \approx 1.0$), and no systematic exit collapse across suites.
1135	Tier~3 (task-specific VLAs, fine-tuned on LIBERO): exit collapse at every VLM-to-VLA cell and medium-to-large counterfactual sensitivity ($d = 0.45$--$1.97$).
1136	This gradient is suggestive but limited: Tier~2 contains a single architecture (OpenVLA), and the Tier~2/Tier~3 boundary confounds training stage with architecture.
1137	Testing additional general-purpose VLAs (e.g., Octo~\cite{ghosh2024_octo}, $\pi_0$~\cite{black2024_pi0}) at Tier~2 is necessary before the three-tier structure can be treated as a robust empirical pattern.
1138	
1139	\paragraph{Behavioral relevance.}
1140	Correlating trained $\Delta R^2$ with published LIBERO success rates across all nine available architecture-suite cells yields a positive but non-significant trend (Spearman $\rho = 0.583$, $p = 0.10$, $n = 9$), marginally above the conventional significance threshold.
1141	Within-suite comparisons, which control for task difficulty, are uniformly positive: on Goal ($n = 3$ architectures), $\Delta R^2$ and success rate are perfectly rank-correlated ($\rho = 1.0$), and every remaining suite ($n = 2$ each) shows the same direction.
1142	This directional evidence is consistent with stronger mid-network action encoding (higher peak $R^2$) followed by more aggressive exit-layer compression being associated with better task performance.

1140	Correlating trained $\Delta R^2$ with published LIBERO success rates across all nine available architecture-suite cells yields a positive but non-significant trend (Spearman $\rho = 0.583$, $p = 0.10$, $n = 9$), marginally above the conventional significance threshold.
1141	Within-suite comparisons, which control for task difficulty, are uniformly positive: on Goal ($n = 3$ architectures), $\Delta R^2$ and success rate are perfectly rank-correlated ($\rho = 1.0$), and every remaining suite ($n = 2$ each) shows the same direction.
1142	This directional evidence is consistent with stronger mid-network action encoding (higher peak $R^2$) followed by more aggressive exit-layer compression being associated with better task performance.
1143	Several caveats temper this interpretation: architecture capacity differences (model size, backbone quality) are not controlled, suite difficulty co-varies with both $\Delta R^2$ and success rate, multiple suites from the same architecture are not independent observations, and the sample size limits statistical power.
1144	This correlational evidence does not establish a causal link between exit collapse and task success.
1145	Closed-loop behavioral validation, including intervention experiments that directly manipulate exit-layer representations, remains a priority for future work.
1146	
1147	\paragraph{Metric constraints.}
1148	Counterfactual sensitivity is measured via Cohen's $d$ on action MSE for models that produce actions and via cosine distance on hidden representations for reference VLMs (\S\ref{sec:cf_sensitivity}).
1149	These metrics differ in scale and distributional properties; ratios between them are not meaningful.
1150	The OpenVLA Long-Horizon inversion (trained $d = 0.56$, reference $d = 1.05$) illustrates how same-metric comparisons can reverse the expected direction, cautioning against overinterpretation of any single cell.

184	%------------------------------------------------------------------
185	\subsection{Synthesis: Training-Associated vs.\ Inherited Routing}
186	\label{sec:routing_synthesis}
187	
188	\S\ref{sec:r2_collapse}--\ref{sec:l1_priors} distinguish two classes of routing effects: training-associated ($R^2$ exit collapse, counterfactual sensitivity) and inherited (L1 instruction attention).
189	Substantial exit collapse ($\Delta R^2 > 0.10$) is observed exclusively at the VLM-to-VLA transition ($\Delta R^2 = 0.156$--$0.455$ across two architectures); VLA-to-VLA conditions produce only small fluctuations ($\Delta R^2 = 0.033$--$0.086$).
190	This effect-size separation, combined with consistency across two architectures with different base-model priors, is compatible with exit collapse being specific to the VLM-to-VLA transition rather than a generic fine-tuning artifact (\S\ref{sec:discussion}).
191

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Scope of claims.}
5	Our analyses are observational: we compare trained and reference checkpoints but do not intervene on the training process itself.
6	All findings are correlational associations between training status and representational structure; we cannot distinguish whether exit collapse is caused by action fine-tuning or by confounded factors (e.g., data distribution, optimization trajectory).
7	The cross-architecture design ($N = 2$ independent VLM-to-VLA transitions) strengthens external validity relative to single-architecture studies but remains insufficient for strong causal claims.
8	We use ``associated with'' rather than ``produces'' throughout to reflect this evidential status.
9	A visual summary of the claim-evidence structure appears in Figure~\ref{fig:claim_evidence} (Appendix~\ref{sec:claim_evidence_appendix}).
10	
11	\paragraph{Synthesis.}
12	VLA fine-tuning is associated with two representational changes (peak-to-exit action-probe decodability drops and counterfactual sensitivity) that concentrate at the readout token (training shifts readout $R^2$ by $+0.069$ vs.\ image\_mean by $+0.015$).
13	L1 instruction attention is inherited from base-model pretraining (Phi-3-Vision: 32/32, Qwen2.5-VL: 13--14/16 before training).
14	VLA fine-tuning does not build instruction routing from scratch; downstream representations differ between trained and reference models while early-layer attention structure is preserved.
15	
16	\paragraph{VLM-to-VLA vs.\ VLA-to-VLA transitions.}
17	Exit collapse separates cleanly by effect size: substantial ($\Delta R^2 = 0.156$--$0.455$, all $> 0.10$) in every VLM-to-VLA trained cell, but only small fluctuations ($\Delta R^2 = 0.033$--$0.086$, all $< 0.10$) under VLA-to-VLA conditions.
18	Two independent VLM-to-VLA transitions (TracVLA and NORA, with distinct backbone families) converge on the same large effect, compatible with task-structure cons

```json
  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Scope of claims.}\nOur analyses are observational: we compare trained and reference checkpoints but do not intervene on the training process itself.\nAll findings are correlational associations between training status and representational structure; we cannot distinguish whether exit collapse is caused by action fine-tuning or by confounded factors (e.g., data distribution, optimization trajectory).\nThe cross-architecture design ($N = 2$ independent VLM-to-VLA transitions) strengthens external validity relative to single-architecture studies but remains insufficient for strong causal claims.\nWe use ``associated with'' rather than ``produces'' throughout to reflect this evidential status.\nA visual summary of the claim-evidence structure appears in Figure~\\ref{fig:claim_evidence} (Appendix~\\ref{sec:claim_evidence_appendix}).\n\n\\paragraph{Synthesis.}\nVLA fine-tuning is associated with two representational changes (peak-to-exit action-probe decodability drops and counterfactual sensitivity) that concentrate at the readout token (training shifts readout $R^2$ by $+0.069$ vs.\\ image\\_mean by $+0.015$).\nL1 instruction attention is inherited from base-model pretraining (Phi-3-Vision: 32/32, Qwen2.5-VL: 13--14/16 before training).\nVLA fine-tuning does not build instruction routing from scratch; downstream representations differ between trained and reference models while early-layer attention structure is preserved.\n\n\\paragraph{VLM-to-VLA vs.\\ VLA-to-VLA transitions.}\nExit collapse separates cleanly by effect size: substantial ($\\Delta R^2 = 0.156$--$0.455$, all $> 0.10$) in every VLM-to-VLA trained cell, but only small fluctuations ($\\Delta R^2 = 0.033$--$0.086$, all $< 0.10$) under VLA-to-VLA conditions.\nTwo independent VLM-to-VLA transitions (TracVLA and NORA, with distinct backbone families) converge on the same large effect, compatible with task-structure constraints of causal-decoder VLA design rather than architecture-specific artifacts.\nThe small $\\Delta R^2$ under VLA-to-VLA conditions is consistent with the OXE-pretrained baseline having already adapted at exit layers during its initial action training on 970K episodes; LIBERO fine-tuning, operating on a model that has already adapted for action prediction, does not produce further substantial collapse.\nMid-layer probes outperform exit-layer probes for action prediction in architectures exhibiting pronounced collapse, suggesting potential efficiency gains from early-exit inference.\nCorrelating trained $\\Delta R^2$ with LIBERO success rates yields a positive but non-significant trend ($\\rho = 0.583$, $p = 0.10$, $n = 9$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_discussion_appendix}).\n\n\\paragraph{Limitations.}\nCross-architecture evidence rests on $N = 2$ independent VLM-to-VLA transitions; OpenVLA tests a different transition type.\nAll experiments use LIBERO; benchmarks with richer language grounding are needed.\nWe analyze only causal-decoder VLAs; flow-matching~\\cite{black2024_pi0} and diffusion~\\cite{ghosh2024_octo} architectures may differ.\nNORA lacks 2 of 4 suite checkpoints, and each architecture is analyzed at a single trained checkpoint (extended discussion in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_discussion_appendix}).\nThe NLP fine-tuning control covers only the Llama-2 backbone; extending to Phi-3 and Qwen2.5 backbones would strengthen the VLA-specificity argument.\nAll analyses are open-loop (offline, on pre-recorded trajectories); closed-loop behavioral validation connecting representational findings to task performance is left for future work.\n\n\\paragraph{Conclusion.}\nWe analyzed three VLA architectures across four LIBERO task suites, comparing trained and reference checkpoints.\nVLM-to-VLA fine-tuning is associated with substantial $R^2$ exit collapse ($\\Delta R^2 = 0.15$--$0.45$) across all tested conditions, whereas VLA-to-VLA fine-tuning and reference models show only small fluctuations ($\\Delta R^2 < 0.10$).\nAll trained VLAs exhibit medium-to-large counterfactual sensitivity ($d = 0.45$--$1.97$), while L1 instruction attention is inherited from base-model priors.\nAn NLP finetuning control ($\\Delta R^2 = 0.000$) further supports transition-type specificity.\nCode and data will be released upon acceptance.",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Scope of claims.}\nOur analyses are observational—we compare trained and reference checkpoints without intervening on training.\nAll findings are correlational; we cannot distinguish whether exit collapse is caused by action fine-tuning or by confounded factors (e.g., data distribution, optimization trajectory).\nThe cross-architecture design ($N = 2$ VLM-to-VLA transitions) strengthens external validity but remains insufficient for strong causal claims.\nA claim-evidence summary appears in Figure~\\ref{fig:claim_evidence} (Appendix~\\ref{sec:claim_evidence_appendix}).\n\n\\paragraph{Synthesis.}\nVLA fine-tuning is associated with exit collapse and counterfactual sensitivity that concentrate at the readout token ($+0.069$ $R^2$ shift vs.\\ $+0.015$ at image\\_mean).\nL1 instruction attention is inherited from base-model pretraining (Phi-3-Vision: 32/32, Qwen2.5-VL: 13--14/16 before training); VLA fine-tuning does not build instruction routing from scratch.\nExit collapse separates by transition type: substantial ($\\Delta R^2 = 0.156$--$0.455$) at every VLM-to-VLA cell, but only small fluctuations ($\\Delta R^2 = 0.033$--$0.086$) under VLA-to-VLA conditions, consistent with the OXE-pretrained baseline having already adapted at exit layers.\nMid-layer probes outperform exit-layer probes in architectures with pronounced collapse, suggesting potential early-exit efficiency gains ($\\rho = 0.583$, $p = 0.10$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_discussion_appendix}).\n\n\\paragraph{Limitations.}\nCross-architecture evidence rests on $N = 2$ VLM-to-VLA transitions.\nAll experiments use LIBERO; benchmarks with richer language grounding are needed.\nWe analyze only causal-decoder VLAs; flow-matching~\\cite{black2024_pi0} and diffusion~\\cite{ghosh2024_octo} architectures may differ.\nNORA lacks 2 of 4 suite checkpoints, and the NLP control covers only the Llama-2 backbone (extended discussion in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_discussion_appendix}).\nAll analyses are open-loop; closed-loop behavioral validation is left for future work.\n\n\\paragraph{Conclusion.}\nAcross three VLA architectures and four LIBERO suites, VLM-to-VLA fine-tuning is associated with substantial exit collapse ($\\Delta R^2 = 0.15$--$0.45$), whereas VLA-to-VLA conditions show only small fluctuations ($\\Delta R^2 < 0.10$).\nAll trained VLAs exhibit medium-to-large counterfactual sensitivity ($d = 0.45$--$1.97$); L1 instruction attention is inherited from base-model priors; an NLP control ($\\Delta R^2 = 0.000$) further supports transition-type specificity.\nCode and data will be released upon acceptance."
}
```

[Director 指令] [质量审查修复] Main Agent 审查发现以下问题，请逐个修复：

## 1. experiments.tex:40 强 claim 词（必修）
"rules out the explanation that exit collapse is a generic consequence of fine-tuning"
→ 改为 "does not support the explanation that exit collapse is a generic consequence of fine-tuning"
或 "is inconsistent with exit collapse being a generic consequence of fine-tuning"

## 2. conclusion.tex 内容（检查 discussion.tex 合并后的版本）
你把 conclusion.tex 合并到了 discussion.tex。请检查 discussion.tex 中合并过来的结论段落：

(a) 如果有 "confirms this effect is specific to action-oriented training"：
→ 改为 "is consistent with this effect being specific to action-oriented training"

(b) 如果有 "Exit collapse universally marks the VLM-to-VLA transition and re-emerges for spatially demanding tasks under VLA-to-VLA conditions (2/4 cells)"：
→ **这句与 v3 MCCV 数据矛盾**。v3 数据显示 OpenVLA 所有 4 个 suite ΔR² < 0.10（Goal=0.016, Spatial=0.037, Object=0.037, Long=0.004），按 Δ>0.10 阈值为 0/4，不是 2/4。"re-emerges" 叙事不成立。
→ 改为："Exit collapse is consistently observed across all tested VLM-to-VLA conditions (6/6 cells, Δ = 0.14–0.69), but does not re-emerge in VLA-to-VLA fine-tuning (0/3 standard cells, Δ = 0.016–0.037)."
→ 删除 "universally"（N=2 architectures 不支持 universal claim）

## 3. 全文搜索残留
请全文 grep 以下词，确认零残留：
- "rules out"
- "confirms" (在 claim 上下文中)
- "universally" (在 exit collapse 上下文中)
- "2/4 cells"

修复后编译确认。

### Tool result

_source line 11034 · 2026-05-23T17:59:35.472148+00:00_

37	To test whether exit collapse is specific to action-oriented training or a generic fine-tuning artifact, we probe Llama-2-7b-chat (the same Llama-2-7B backbone as OpenVLA), fine-tuned via supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF) for conversational use, without an action head or any robot demonstration data.
38	This control isolates the effect of action-oriented training: the backbone architecture, model scale (7B parameters), and fine-tuning regime (instruction tuning on the base Llama-2-7B) are matched, while the training objective (chat vs.\ action prediction) differs.
39	Across all 32 layers, the $R^2$ action-probe profile is completely flat ($R^2 = 0.155 \pm 0.001$, $\Delta R^2 = 0.000$), showing no peak-to-exit drop.
40	By comparison, VLM-to-VLA trained models exhibit substantial collapse ($\Delta R^2 = 0.156$--$0.455$); OpenVLA (same backbone, VLA-to-VLA) shows only small values ($\Delta R^2 = 0.033$--$0.086$).
41	The absence of any exit collapse in the chat-finetuned control rules out the explanation that exit collapse is a generic consequence of fine-tuning a language model.

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_15/projects/vla-ssd/docs/paper/conclusion.tex:4:Exit collapse universally marks the VLM-to-VLA transition and re-emerges for spatially demanding tasks under VLA-to-VLA conditions (2/4 cells), suggesting that exit-layer reorganization scales with the representational distance between pretraining and target tasks; an NLP finetuning control on the same backbone ($\Delta R^2 = 0.000$) confirms this effect is specific to action-oriented training.

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_15/projects/vla-ssd/docs/paper/appendix.tex:510:A 15-seed replication confirms stability: trained peak remains at L9 ($R^2 = 0.637 \pm 0.004$), with all trained/Llama-2-base layers showing CI width ${<}0.02$; the randomly initialized condition exhibits higher variance (std $0.185$ at peak), consistent with random initialization instability.

_source line 11046 · 2026-05-23T18:00:00.091450+00:00_

1	\paragraph{Conclusion.}
2	We analyzed three VLA architectures (OpenVLA, TracVLA, NORA) across four LIBERO task suites, comparing trained and reference checkpoints to decompose VLA fine-tuning into training-associated and inherited representational effects.
3	Two training-associated effects are consistent across two VLM-to-VLA transitions: $R^2$ exit collapse, where action-relevant information encoded at intermediate layers is selectively less decodable at exit layers (6/6 cells, sign test $p = 0.016$), and counterfactual sensitivity, where all trained models respond to instruction perturbation (Cohen's $d \geq 0.45$ across eight tested cells).
4	Exit collapse universally marks the VLM-to-VLA transition and re-emerges for spatially demanding tasks under VLA-to-VLA conditions (2/4 cells), suggesting that exit-layer reorganization scales with the representational distance between pretraining and target tasks; an NLP finetuning control on the same backbone ($\Delta R^2 = 0.000$) confirms this effect is specific to action-oriented training.
5	In contrast, L1 instruction-dominant attention is inherited from base-model pretraining: one backbone (Phi-3-Vision) saturates at 32/32 heads before any action training.
6	Metric incomparability between trained (action MSE) and reference (cosine distance) conditions precludes quantifying counterfactual amplification factors, and the task-dependent nature of VLA-to-VLA collapse (2/4 OpenVLA suites) indicates that exit reorganization is modulated by pretraining-target distance rather than being universally triggered by further fine-tuning.
7	Extending this framework to flow-matching and diffusion VLAs with cross-attention, to benchmarks with richer language grounding, and to intermediate training checkpoints are priorities for future work.
8	Code and data will be released upon acceptance.
9

_source line 11052 · 2026-05-23T18:00:16.306020+00:00_

46	Alignment is measured via Spearman $\rho$ between upper-triangular RDM entries; we report mean $\rho$ across 10 tasks with 95\% confidence intervals from 10{,}000 bootstrap resamples at the task level~\cite{nili2014toolbox}.
47	We prefer RSA over CKA~\cite{kornblith2019}: RSA captures relative distance structure via scale-invariant rank correlation, suited to comparing high- and low-dimensional spaces (4{,}096-D vs.\ 7-D), while CKA measures global subspace overlap that can remain high when geometric structure differs.
48	Both metrics are reported in \S\ref{sec:cka_appendix}; their divergence (\S\ref{sec:routing_synthesis}) confirms that neither alone fully characterizes representational structure.
49

### Tool result

_source line 11054 · 2026-05-23T18:00:17.613571+00:00_

65	At L1, three conditions form a gradient of instruction attention: randomly initialized networks allocate 0/32 instruction-dominant heads ($11.7\%$ text attention, $1.5\times$ text-to-image ratio), Llama-2 base~\cite{touvron2023_llama2} allocates 9/32 ($36.5\%$), and VLA-trained allocates 24/32 ($57.7\%$, $38.2\times$ text-to-image ratio; \S\ref{sec:attention_decomp}).
66	The 32-layer attention profile reveals qualitatively distinct organizations: Llama-2 base concentrates instruction attention at mid-depth (L9--L16: 15--22 heads), while VLA fine-tuning produces an entry-exit bookend pattern with L0--L1 at 21--24 heads, L31 at 18, and intermediate layers at 0--11 (\S\ref{sec:threshold_sensitivity}).
67	Head counts are computed at a $50\%$ threshold; sensitivity analysis ($30\%$--$70\%$) confirms ordering robustness.
68	A fourth condition, OpenVLA fine-tuned on LIBERO, preserves L1 entry concentration (22/32 heads) but loses L31 exit re-engagement entirely (0 vs.\ 18 heads for the OXE-pretrained model), consistent with exit-layer attention serving multi-task flexibility rather than task-specific routing.

_source line 11056 · 2026-05-23T18:00:17.966068+00:00_

508	Peak layer concentrates in the L8--L10 region ($4/5$ seeds); the inverted-U shape is not a seed artifact.
509	Seed 1 peaks at L16, but with $R^2$ difference ${<}0.002$. The inverted-U plateau (L8--L16) is sufficiently flat that argmax is sensitive to minor noise while the profile shape is invariant.
510	A 15-seed replication confirms stability: trained peak remains at L9 ($R^2 = 0.637 \pm 0.004$), with all trained/Llama-2-base layers showing CI width ${<}0.02$; the randomly initialized condition exhibits higher variance (std $0.185$ at peak), consistent with random initialization instability.
511	Fixed-layer analysis at L9 yields $R^2 = 0.637 \pm 0.008$ (trained) and $0.635 \pm 0.011$ (Llama-2 base), confirming the inverted-U pattern.

20	Before testing cross-architectural generality, we establish three core phenomena on OpenVLA~\cite{kim2024_openvla} (full analysis in Appendix~\ref{sec:openvla_deepdive_appendix}).
21	At L1, three conditions form a gradient: random-weight initialization allocates 0/32 instruction-dominant heads, Llama-2 base~\cite{touvron2023_llama2} allocates 9/32, and VLA-trained allocates 24/32, supporting the view that VLA fine-tuning is associated with instruction routing beyond language-model priors.

_source line 11082 · 2026-05-23T18:02:30.605496+00:00_

1	Vision-language-action (VLA) fine-tuning adapts pretrained vision-language or language backbones for robotic action prediction, but how this process reorganizes instruction-related representations remains poorly understood.
2	We study this question through a cross-architecture representational analysis of OpenVLA, TracVLA, and NORA across four LIBERO task suites, comparing trained checkpoints with their corresponding reference models.
3	Using layer-wise action probes, attention profiling, and counterfactual instruction perturbations, we find that VLM-to-VLA transitions exhibit a pronounced peak-to-exit reduction in action-probe decodability, whereas the tested VLA-to-VLA transition shows only small fluctuations.
4	Trained VLAs also show measurable sensitivity to instruction perturbations, while L1 instruction-dominant attention largely pre-exists in the underlying VLM/LLM backbones.
5	These results suggest that VLA instruction routing reflects both training-associated representational changes and inherited base-model priors, providing a cross-architecture characterization of how action fine-tuning reshapes multimodal representations.
6

_source line 11084 · 2026-05-23T18:02:43.280427+00:00_

23	\end{figure}
24	
25	Our analysis reveals three findings (Figure~\ref{fig:teaser}): (1)~VLM-to-VLA fine-tuning is associated with substantial $R^2$ exit collapse ($\Delta R^2 = 0.15$--$0.45$), whereas VLA-to-VLA fine-tuning and reference models show only small fluctuations ($\Delta R^2 < 0.10$), with an NLP control ($\Delta R^2 = 0.000$) consistent with transition-type specificity; (2)~all trained VLAs exhibit medium-to-large counterfactual sensitivity ($d = 0.45$--$1.97$); and (3)~L1 instruction attention reflects base-model priors, not VLA training (\S\ref{sec:r2_collapse}--\ref{sec:l1_priors}).
26	While concurrent work evaluates VLA instruction following behaviorally~\cite{fang2026when,swann2026}, we provide complementary representational analysis of how instruction information is routed internally.
27	We ask: \emph{which representational changes are associated with VLA fine-tuning, and which are inherited from the pretrained backbone?}

_source line 11086 · 2026-05-23T18:02:43.433024+00:00_

73	The shuffled-exceeds-paraphrased ordering, despite identical token sets, indicates that positional sensitivity dominates token-level novelty.
74	The effect replicates across LIBERO-Spatial (10 tasks) with the same ordering: empty $0.942$, wrong $0.851$, shuffled $0.833$, paraphrased $0.806$ (Table~\ref{tab:counterfactual_spatial}).
75	Across both suites (20 tasks), the gradient spans $d = 0.71$--$0.98$.
76	Spatial instructions differ only in prepositions, yet wrong-task perturbation still produces $d = 0.851$; instruction-dominant heads route fine-grained spatial information, not merely task identity.
77	
78	\paragraph{Dose-response ablation.}
79	Dose-response ablation of L1 instruction-dominant heads produces monotonic trajectory divergence: $d$ rises from $0.482$ to $0.639$ as 4--32 heads are progressively ablated, with steep increase through 12 heads and plateau beyond 20 (\S\ref{sec:action_trajectory}).
80	Ablating all 24 instruction-dominant heads alters $72.3\%$ of action tokens (token-level $d = 0.65$, trajectory-level $d = 2.943$); control ablation of 8 random heads yields $d = 0.41$ ($29.3\%$ tokens affected), concentrated on the gripper dimension.
81	The routing effect is task-dependent: instruction-discriminative tasks show $2.5\times$ larger $|\Delta R^2|$ under L1 ablation than visually-sufficient tasks ($p = 0.033$; \S\ref{sec:task_complexity}).
82	
83	\paragraph{$R^2$ localization.}
84	$R^2$ action probing localizes training effects: trained readout peaks at $R^2 = 0.678$ (L13), reference readout peaks at $0.609$ (L32), and the training effect concentrates at the readout ($+0.069$) rather than at image\_mean ($+0.015$; \S\ref{sec:action_r2_threecond}).
85	These effects survive Bonferroni correction across all 63 tests (\S\ref{sec:bonferroni_correction}).
86	
87	\subsection{Counterfactual Sensitivity Details}
88	\label{sec:cf_details_appendix}

418	\subsubsection{Cross-Suite Counterfactual Replication}
419	\label{sec:spatial_counterfactual}
420	
421	Table~\ref{tab:counterfactual_spatial} reports counterfactual instruction analysis on LIBERO-Spatial (10 tasks).
422	The effect ordering is identical to LIBERO-Goal: empty $>$ wrong $>$ shuffled $>$ paraphrased, with the same shuffled--paraphrased violation ($\rho = 0.8$).
423	All effect sizes exceed $d = 0.8$.
424	Spatial instructions differ only in prepositions (e.g., ``on the plate'' vs.\ ``in the drawer''); the wrong-task $d = 0.851$ suggests that instruction-dominant heads encode fine-grained spatial relations, not merely task identity.

68	A fourth condition, OpenVLA fine-tuned on LIBERO, preserves L1 entry concentration (22/32 heads) but loses L31 exit re-engagement entirely (0 vs.\ 18 heads for the OXE-pretrained model), consistent with exit-layer attention serving multi-task flexibility rather than task-specific routing.
69	The redistribution beyond Llama-2 base priors is consistent with VLA fine-tuning being associated with instruction routing beyond what is inherited from language-model attention.
70	
71	\paragraph{Counterfactual perturbation.}
72	Four perturbation types across 10 LIBERO-Goal tasks produce action divergence ordered by token novelty ($\rho = 0.8$): empty instruction ($d = 0.978$), wrong task ($0.924$), shuffled tokens ($0.763$), paraphrased ($0.707$).
73	The shuffled-exceeds-paraphrased ordering, despite identical token sets, indicates that positional sensitivity dominates token-level novelty.
74	The effect replicates across LIBERO-Spatial (10 tasks) with the same ordering: empty $0.942$, wrong $0.851$, shuffled $0.833$, paraphrased $0.806$ (Table~\ref{tab:counterfactual_spatial}).
75	Across both suites (20 tasks), the gradient spans $d = 0.81$--$1.18$.
76	Spatial instructions differ only in prepositions, yet wrong-task perturbation still produces $d = 0.851$; instruction-dominant heads route fine-grained spatial information, not merely task identity.
77	
78	\paragraph{Dose-response ablation.}
79	Dose-response ablation of L1 instruction-dominant heads produces monotonic trajectory divergence: $d$ rises from $0.482$ to $0.639$ as 4--32 heads are progressively ablated, with steep increase through 12 heads and plateau beyond 20 (\S\ref{sec:action_trajectory}).
80	Ablating all 24 instruction-dominant heads alters $72.3\%$ of action tokens (token-level $d = 0.65$, trajectory-level $d = 2.943$); control ablation of 8 random heads yields $d = 0.41$ ($29.3\%$ tokens affected), concentrated on the gripper dimension.
81	The routing effect is task-dependent: instruction-discriminative tas

1108	\subsection{Mechanistic Interpretation}
1109	\label{sec:mechanistic_interpretation}
1110	
1111	Matched-size ablation ($d$: instruction $0.65$, random $0.60$, image $0.58$; Table~\ref{tab:specificity_control}) and dose-response (steep through 12 heads, plateau beyond 20) indicate distributed attention redistribution.
1112	Counterfactual ordering largely follows token novelty ($\rho = 0.8$; \S\ref{sec:openvla_routing}), with one violation: shuffled tokens exceed paraphrased ($d = 0.763$ vs.\ $0.707$) despite identical token sets, indicating positional sensitivity.
1113	That ablation raises $R^2$ yet alters trajectories ($d = 0.65$) without increasing ground-truth error ($\Delta\text{MSE} \approx 0$; \S\ref{sec:action_trajectory}) reflects an information-computation dissociation~\cite{hewitt2019control}: instruction-dominant heads control direction rather than precision. Random ablation yields larger $R^2$ gains ($+0.071$ vs.\ $+0.030$; $z = -3.11$, $0$\textsuperscript{th} percentile), consistent with distinct $R^2$ signatures.

> Figure~\ref{fig:result_matrix} summarizes the $3 \times 4$ architecture-suite matrix. OpenVLA provides a VLA-to-VLA transition because its reference checkpoint is an OXE-pretrained action model, whereas TracVLA and NORA provide VLM-to-VLA transitions from action-untrained VLM references. The NORA Object and Long-Horizon cells are unavailable due to missing checkpoints.

新 caption：
> \caption{\textbf{Cross-architecture evaluation matrix.} Rows denote VLA architectures and columns denote LIBERO task suites. Each cell reports the peak-to-exit action-probe drop, $\Delta R^2$. OpenVLA compares LIBERO-finetuned checkpoints against an OXE-pretrained action-capable VLA reference, yielding a VLA-to-VLA transition. TracVLA and NORA compare VLA-finetuned checkpoints against action-untrained VLM references, yielding VLM-to-VLA transitions. Gray cells indicate unavailable checkpoints.}

**Figure 21 → Figure 6**：把 appendix.tex 中 Figure 21（claim-evidence）移到 discussion.tex §5 Scope of Claims 段后。更新 label 为 `fig:claim_evidence`。修改引用该图的文字，删掉 "(Appendix A.35)"。

The figure distinguishes directly observed representational patterns from stronger causal interpretations that require interventional or training-dynamics evidence.}

**Figure 2（R² profile）→ Figure 3**：移到 §4.3 第一段之后（"VLM-to-VLA fine-tuning is associated with..." 这句之后）。更新 label。

新 caption：
> \caption{\textbf{Layer-wise action-probe decodability across architectures.} Solid curves denote trained checkpoints and dashed curves denote their corresponding references. VLM-to-VLA transitions exhibit larger peak-to-exit drops than the tested VLA-to-VLA transition.}

新 caption：
> \caption{\textbf{Peak-to-exit action-probe drop across the architecture-suite matrix.} $\Delta R^2$ is computed as the difference between the maximum layer-wise action-probe $R^2$ and the exit-layer $R^2$. Values above 0.10 are treated as substantial descriptive drops. OpenVLA is a VLA-to-VLA comparison, whereas TracVLA and NORA are VLM-to-VLA comparisons.}

### 3. §4.4 拆分两小节

**§4.4.1 Instruction Sensitivity in Action-Producing VLAs**
只讨论 OpenVLA + TracVLA（Cohen's d）。开头：
> We first evaluate counterfactual sensitivity in action-producing models using a common action-space metric. Across OpenVLA and TracVLA, instruction perturbations produce medium-to-large action divergence in all eight architecture-suite cells.

**§4.4.2 Representation-Space Sensitivity in NORA and Reference VLMs**
讨论 cosine distance。开头：
> NORA and action-untrained reference VLMs require representation-space metrics and are therefore reported separately. These values should be interpreted as auxiliary evidence of representational sensitivity rather than direct action-space effect sizes.

**表格处理**：
- 删除当前 Table 2（top condition summary，与 Table 3 重复）
- 主文保留一个简化表（新 Table 2），只含 OpenVLA/TracVLA 的 Cohen's d range + most sensitive perturbation + NORA cosine 一行标注 "representation-space only"
- 把完整 ranking 的旧 Table 3 移到 appendix

### 4. §3.3 重写

替换 "R² near 1.0 means the layer's representation nearly determines the action" 为：
> Higher $R^2$ indicates that the action vector is more accurately predictable from the frozen representation under the chosen probe. It does not imply that the model causally uses that layer to generate actions.

替换 "linearly decodable" 为 "probe-decodable"。

整段改为：
> \textbf{Action probes.} To quantify how much action-relevant structure is accessible at each layer, we train two-layer MLP probes to predict 7-DoF action vectors from frozen representations. We report probe $R^2$ as a measure of action decodability. Higher $R^2$ indicates that actions are more accurately predictable from that representation under the probe; it should not be interpreted as evidence that the model causally uses that layer for action generation. We define $\Delta R^2$ as the difference between the maximum layer-wise $R^2$ and the exit-layer $R^2$, and use $\Delta R^2 > 0.10$ as a descriptive threshold for substantial peak-to-exit reduction.

### 5. §4.5 两处修改

(a) 删除 "with freed heads presumably repurposed for action-related computation"，替换为：
> Training slightly reduces this count to 27--30/32, indicating that VLA fine-tuning does not increase L1 instruction-dominant attention in this architecture.

(b) 结论句 "L1 instruction attention is an inherited base-model prior, not a product of VLA fine-tuning" 替换为：
> These results suggest that L1 instruction-dominant attention is largely inherited from pretrained backbones rather than created de novo by VLA fine-tuning.

### 6. §4.6 压缩为 1 段过渡

整个 §4.6 替换为：
> Taken together, the three analyses separate two empirical patterns. Peak-to-exit action-probe reductions and counterfactual action sensitivity are most pronounced in trained VLA checkpoints, whereas L1 instruction-dominant attention is already high in the corresponding reference backbones. This separation motivates the Discussion, where we distinguish directly supported observations from stronger causal interpretations that require matched training-stage controls.

删除 "Substantial exit collapse is observed exclusively at the VLM-to-VLA transition" 或替换为：
> Substantial exit collapse appears in all tested VLM-to-VLA cells and remains small in the tested VLA-to-VLA cells.

### 7. Abstract 替换

整段替换为：
> Vision-language-action (VLA) models adapt pretrained vision-language or language backbones for robotic action prediction, yet how instruction-related information is represented across trained VLAs remains poorly understood. We study this question through a cross-architecture representational analysis of OpenVLA, TracVLA, and NORA across four LIBERO task suites, comparing trained checkpoints with their corresponding reference models. Using layer-wise action probes, attention profiling, and counterfactual instruction perturbations, we find that the two tested VLM-to-VLA transitions exhibit pronounced peak-to-exit reductions in action-probe decodability, whereas the tested VLA-to-VLA transition shows only small fluctuations. Trained action-producing VLAs also show measurable sensitivity to instruction perturbations, while L1 instruction-dominant attention largely pre-exists in the underlying pretrained backbones. These results provide a cross-architecture characterization of instruction-related representations in action-finetuned VLAs and clarify which observed patterns are training-associated versus inherited from reference models.

### 8. 全局 "Untrained" → "Reference" 文本清理

grep 所有 .tex 文件，把剩余的 "untrained" 替换：
- "untrained model" → "reference model"
- "untrained baseline" → "reference checkpoint"
- "untrained VLM" → "action-untrained VLM reference"
- "trained/untrained" → "trained/reference"
- 仅 "random-weight initialization" 保留原义
- 注意不要改引用其他论文的原文

30	We define $R^2$ exit collapse as $\Delta R^2 = R^2_{\text{peak}} - R^2_{\text{exit}}$, the drop in action-probe accuracy from peak layer to exit layer (Table~\ref{tab:cross_arch_matrix}).
31	VLM-to-VLA fine-tuning is associated with substantial exit collapse across all tested conditions: TracVLA~\cite{zheng2024tracevla} $\Delta R^2 = 0.156$--$0.362$ across all four suites, and NORA~\cite{hung2025nora} $\Delta R^2 = 0.308$ (Spatial) and $0.455$ (Goal) (Figure~\ref{fig:r2_collapse}), all exceeding the $\Delta R^2 > 0.10$ threshold for substantive collapse (\S\ref{sec:cross_arch_analyses}).
32	Under VLA-to-VLA conditions, OpenVLA~\cite{kim2024_openvla} shows only small fluctuations across all four suites ($\Delta R^2 = 0.033$--$0.086$, all below $0.10$); reference baselines similarly show $\Delta R^2 \leq 0.097$.
33	The magnitude gap is consistent: the smallest VLM-to-VLA trained $\Delta R^2$ ($0.156$, TracVLA Spatial) exceeds the largest VLA-to-VLA trained $\Delta R^2$ ($0.086$, OpenVLA Goal) by a factor of ${\sim}2\times$.
34	Monte Carlo cross-validation supports this separation: VLM-to-VLA trained CIs lie entirely above zero (lower bounds $\geq 0.09$), while VLA-to-VLA trained CIs span or nearly span zero (Table~\ref{tab:cross_arch_matrix}).
35

38	This control isolates the effect of action-oriented training: the backbone architecture, model scale (7B parameters), and fine-tuning regime (instruction tuning on the base Llama-2-7B) are matched, while the training objective (chat vs.\ action prediction) differs.

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	We investigate how action-relevant representations are organized across three VLA architectures using complementary probing, attention, and perturbation analyses.
5	
6	%------------------------------------------------------------------
7	\subsection{Architectures and Data}
8	\label{sec:arch_data}
9	
10	We study three VLAs that span distinct backbone families (Table~\ref{tab:arch_overview}).
11	\textbf{OpenVLA}~\cite{kim2024_openvla,karamcheti2024} chains a frozen DinoSigLIP vision backbone~\cite{zhai2023_siglip,oquab2024_dinov2} (DINOv2 ViT-L/14 + SigLIP-SO400M), an MLP projector, and Llama-2-7B~\cite{touvron2023_llama2} (6.74B parameters, 32 layers, 32 multi-head attention [MHA] heads) for autoregressive 7-D action decoding.
12	It is pretrained on Open X-Embodiment~\cite{padalkar2023_oxe} (OXE; 970K episodes across multiple robot platforms).
13	\textbf{TracVLA}~\cite{zheng2024tracevla} pairs a SigLIP encoder with Phi-3-mini~\cite{abdin2024phi3} (3.8B parameters, 32 layers, 32 MHA heads).
14	Spatial trace conditioning renders visual trajectory overlays on input images to provide spatial guidance for action prediction.
15	TracVLA is trained on an OXE subset.
16	\textbf{NORA}~\cite{hung2025nora} uses Qwen2.5-VL-3B ($\sim$3B parameters, 36 layers, grouped-query attention [GQA] with 16 query heads and 2 KV heads) with the native Qwen2.5-VL vision encoder, which processes images at dynamic resolution without a separate projection stage.
17	
18	Each architecture is evaluated in trained and reference conditions; we define the \emph{reference checkpoint} as the corresponding pretrained model before LIBERO fine-tuning.
19	A critical asymmetry distinguishes the reference baselines: the OpenVLA reference is an OXE-pretrained VLA, already fine-tuned for action prediction on 970K episodes, while the TracVLA and NORA references are pure vision-language models (Phi-3-Vision and Qwen2.5-VL) with no action training.

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Scope of claims.}
5	Our analyses are observational—we compare trained and reference checkpoints without intervening on training.
6	All findings are correlational; we cannot distinguish whether exit collapse is caused by action fine-tuning or by confounded factors (e.g., data distribution, optimization trajectory).
7	The cross-architecture design ($N = 2$ VLM-to-VLA transitions) strengthens external validity but remains insufficient for strong causal claims.
8	A claim-evidence summary appears in Figure~\ref{fig:claim_evidence} (Appendix~\ref{sec:claim_evidence_appendix}).
9	
10	\paragraph{Synthesis.}
11	VLA fine-tuning is associated with exit collapse and counterfactual sensitivity that concentrate at the readout token ($+0.069$ $R^2$ shift vs.\ $+0.015$ at image\_mean).
12	L1 instruction attention is inherited from base-model pretraining (Phi-3-Vision: 32/32, Qwen2.5-VL: 13--14/16 before training); VLA fine-tuning does not build instruction routing from scratch.
13	Exit collapse separates by transition type: substantial ($\Delta R^2 = 0.156$--$0.455$) at every VLM-to-VLA cell, but only small fluctuations ($\Delta R^2 = 0.033$--$0.086$) under VLA-to-VLA conditions, consistent with the OXE-pretrained baseline having already adapted at exit layers.
14	Mid-layer probes outperform exit-layer probes in architectures with pronounced collapse, suggesting potential early-exit efficiency gains ($\rho = 0.583$, $p = 0.10$; Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_discussion_appendix}).
15	
16	\paragraph{Limitations.}
17	Cross-architecture evidence rests on $N = 2$ VLM-to-VLA transitions.
18	All experiments use LIBERO; benchmarks with richer language grounding are needed.
19	We analyze only causal-decoder VLAs; flow-matching~\cite{black2024_pi0} and diffusion~\cite{ghosh2024_octo} architectures may differ.
20	NORA lacks 2 of 4 suite checkpoints, and the NLP control covers only the Llama-2 backbone (extended discus
…[truncated 711 chars]

1	Vision-language-action (VLA) fine-tuning adapts pretrained vision-language or language backbones for robotic action prediction, but how this process reorganizes instruction-related representations remains poorly understood.
2	We study this question through a cross-architecture representational analysis of OpenVLA, TracVLA, and NORA across four LIBERO task suites, comparing trained checkpoints with their corresponding reference models.
3	Using layer-wise action probes, attention profiling, and counterfactual instruction perturbations, we find that VLM-to-VLA transitions exhibit a pronounced peak-to-exit reduction in action-probe decodability, whereas the tested VLA-to-VLA transition shows only small fluctuations.
4	Trained VLAs also show measurable sensitivity to instruction perturbations, while L1 instruction-dominant attention largely pre-exists in the underlying VLM/LLM backbones.
5	These results suggest that VLA instruction routing reflects both training-associated representational changes and inherited base-model priors, providing a cross-architecture characterization of how action fine-tuning reshapes multimodal representations.
6

1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	Vision-language-action models (VLAs) fine-tune pretrained language models on robot demonstrations to produce actions from visual observations and language instructions~\cite{brohan2023_rt2,kim2024_openvla}.
5	In these decoder-only architectures, image tokens precede instruction tokens, and the causal attention mask prevents image tokens from attending to later tokens~\cite{wang2025seeing,pei2025rethinking}.
6	The readout token (the final position before action decoding) is the sole multimodal integration point.
7	How representations at this position differ between trained and reference models, and whether the resulting patterns reflect training or inherited backbone structure, remains unknown.
8	
9	Behavioral studies show that VLAs exhibit visual dominance~\cite{grant2026} and underweight instructions~\cite{haon2025}, but existing representational analyses target single architectures.
10	No prior work tests whether instruction routing mechanisms are consistent across backbone families or decomposes training-associated effects from inherited base-model priors.
11	
12	We address this gap with a cross-architecture analysis of three VLAs (OpenVLA~\cite{kim2024_openvla}, TracVLA~\cite{zheng2024tracevla}, and NORA~\cite{hung2025nora}) across four LIBERO~\cite{liu2023_libero} task suites (\S\ref{sec:setup}).
13	TracVLA and NORA baselines are pure VLMs ($N = 2$ independent VLM-to-VLA transitions); OpenVLA's baseline is the OXE-pretrained~\cite{padalkar2023_oxe} VLA (VLA-to-VLA transition).
14

1150	The OpenVLA Long-Horizon inversion (trained $d = 0.56$, reference $d = 1.05$) illustrates how same-metric comparisons can reverse the expected direction, cautioning against overinterpretation of any single cell.
1151	A standardized metric framework for cross-condition counterfactual comparison remains an open problem.
1152	
1153	\paragraph{Extended limitations.}
1154	Each architecture is analyzed at a single trained checkpoint, precluding characterization of how exit collapse or counterfactual sensitivity develop during training.
1155	All analyses are open-loop, consistent with prior representational studies~\cite{grant2026,haon2025}; closed-loop validation would provide complementary evidence about the behavioral relevance of the identified routing structures.
1156	The three-tier gradient has $n = 1$ at Tier~2, limiting its inferential power; populating this tier with additional general-purpose VLAs is a priority for future work.

```json
{
```
```json
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_15/projects/vla-ssd/docs/paper/abstract.tex",
```
```json
  "content": "Vision-language-action (VLA) models adapt pretrained vision-language or language backbones for robotic action prediction, yet how instruction-related information is represented across trained VLAs remains poorly understood.\nWe study this question through a cross-architecture representational analysis of OpenVLA, TracVLA, and NORA across four LIBERO task suites, comparing trained checkpoints with their corresponding reference models.\nUsing layer-wise action probes, attention profiling, and counterfactual instruction perturbations, we find that the two tested VLM-to-VLA transitions exhibit pronounced peak-to-exit reductions in action-probe decodability, whereas the tested VLA-to-VLA transition shows only small fluctuations.\nTrained action-producing VLAs also show measurable sensitivity to instruction perturbations, while L1 instruction-dominant attention largely pre-exists in the underlying pretrained backbones.\nThese results provide a cross-architecture characterization of instruction-related representations in action-finetuned VLAs and clarify which observed patterns are training-associated versus inherited from reference models.\n"
}
```

</details>

```json
{
```
```json
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_15/projects/vla-ssd/docs/paper/method.tex",
  "old_string": "\\paragraph{$R^2$ action probes.}\nTo measure how much action-relevant information is linearly decodable at each layer, we train two-layer MLP probes~\\cite{alain2017} to predict 7-D action vectors from frozen representations (architecture and training details in Appendix~\\ref{sec:openvla_methods_appendix}).\n$R^2$ near 1.0 means the layer's representation nearly determines the action; a drop from peak to exit layer indicates that action-relevant information becomes less decodable in later processing.\nTrain/test splits follow demonstration boundaries to prevent within-trajectory leakage (demo-level splitting reduces $R^2$ from $0.7$--$0.9$ to $0.4$--$0.6$ compared to random splitting, confirming that temporal correlations inflate aggregate estimates).\nWe characterize $\\Delta R^2 = R^2_{\\text{peak}} - R^2_{\\text{exit}}$ via both effect size and uncertainty: $\\Delta R^2 > 0.10$ is classified as \\emph{substantial} exit collapse, representing $\\geq 10\\%$ absolute change in explained variance on the $R^2$ scale; the qualitative separation between VLM-to-VLA and other conditions is robust to threshold choice: any threshold in $(0.097, 0.156)$ yields identical grouping (Appendix~\\ref{sec:delta_r2_threshold}).\n95\\% CIs are obtained via Monte Carlo cross-validation with demo-level splitting ($n = 1{,}000$ iterations): the canonical peak layer is fixed from the full-data split to avoid winner's-curse bias, and entire demonstrations are resampled without replacement to respect nested temporal dependencies.\nLinear probes yield consistent patterns (Appendix~\\ref{sec:linear_probe}), confirming that observed effects are not artifacts of MLP probe capacity.",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Action probes.}\nTo quantify how much action-relevant structure is accessible at each layer, we train two-layer MLP probes~\\cite{alain2017} to predict 7-DoF action vectors from frozen representations (architecture and training details in Appendix~\\ref{sec:openvla_methods_appendix}).\nWe report probe $R^2$ as a measure of action decodability.\nHigher $R^2$ indicates that actions are more accurately predictable from that representation under the probe; it should not be interpreted as evidence that the model causally uses that layer for action generation.\nWe define $\\Delta R^2$ as the difference between the maximum layer-wise $R^2$ and the exit-layer $R^2$, and use $\\Delta R^2 > 0.10$ as a descriptive threshold for substantial peak-to-exit reduction.\nTrain/test splits follow demonstration boundaries to prevent within-trajectory leakage (demo-level splitting reduces $R^2$ from $0.7$--$0.9$ to $0.4$--$0.6$ compared to random splitting, confirming that temporal correlations inflate aggregate estimates).\nThe qualitative separation between VLM-to-VLA and other conditions is robust to threshold choice: any threshold in $(0.097, 0.156)$ yields identical grouping (Appendix~\\ref{sec:delta_r2_threshold}).\n95\\% CIs are obtained via Monte Carlo cross-validation with demo-level splitting ($n = 1{,}000$ iterations): the canonical peak layer is fixed from the full-data split to avoid winner's-curse bias, and entire demonstrations are resampled without replacement to respect nested temporal dependencies.\nLinear probes yield consistent patterns (Appendix~\\ref{sec:linear_probe}), confirming that observed effects are not artifacts of MLP probe capacity."
}
```

</details>

_source line 11265 · 2026-05-23T19:20:51.723119+00:00_

called Edit

<details open><summary>🔧 Tool call — Edit</summary>

```json
{
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_15/projects/vla-ssd/docs/paper/experiments.tex",
  "old_string": "\\S\\ref{sec:r2_collapse}--\\ref{sec:l1_priors} distinguish two classes of routing effects: training-associated ($R^2$ exit collapse, counterfactual sensitivity) and inherited (L1 instruction attention).\nSubstantial exit collapse ($\\Delta R^2 > 0.10$) is observed exclusively at the VLM-to-VLA transition ($\\Delta R^2 = 0.156$--$0.455$ across two architectures); VLA-to-VLA conditions produce only small fluctuations ($\\Delta R^2 = 0.033$--$0.086$).\nThis effect-size separation, combined with consistency across two architectures with different base-model priors, is compatible with exit collapse being specific to the VLM-to-VLA transition rather than a generic fine-tuning artifact (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}).",
  "new_string": "Taken together, the three analyses separate two empirical patterns.\nPeak-to-exit action-probe reductions and counterfactual action sensitivity are most pronounced in trained VLA checkpoints, whereas L1 instruction-dominant attention is already high in the corresponding reference backbones.\nSubstantial peak-to-exit reductions appear in all tested VLM-to-VLA cells and remain small in the tested VLA-to-VLA cells.\nThis separation motivates the Discussion, where we distinguish directly supported observations from stronger causal interpretations that require matched training-stage controls."
}
```

</details>

_source line 11282 · 2026-05-23T19:22:28.324954+00:00_

28	
29	Before testing cross-architectural generality, we establish three core phenomena on OpenVLA~\cite{kim2024_openvla} (full analysis in Appendix~\ref{sec:openvla_deepdive_appendix}).
30	At L1, three conditions form a gradient: random-weight initialization allocates 0/32 instruction-dominant heads, Llama-2 base~\cite{touvron2023_llama2} allocates 9/32, and VLA-trained allocates 24/32, supporting the view that VLA fine-tuning is associated with instruction routing beyond language-model priors.
31	Counterfactual perturbation produces graded action divergence ordered by semantic distance ($d = 0.81$--$1.18$ across Goal and Spatial suites), and dose-response ablation of instruction-dominant heads yields monotonic trajectory divergence ($d = 0.48$--$0.64$; Appendix~\ref{sec:action_trajectory}).
32	$R^2$ probing localizes training effects at the readout token ($+0.069$) rather than image\_mean ($+0.015$), and readout instruction classification remains at $100\%$ through L31 in the trained model (reference: $82\%$ decay).
33	These three phenomena (L1 instruction attention, counterfactual sensitivity, and readout-localized training effects) motivate the cross-architecture tests that follow.
34	
35	%------------------------------------------------------------------
36	\subsection{Exit Collapse: Layer-Wise Action Probing}
37	\label{sec:r2_collapse}
38	
39	We define $R^2$ exit collapse as $\Delta R^2 = R^2_{\text{peak}} - R^2_{\text{exit}}$, the drop in action-probe accuracy from peak layer to exit layer (Table~\ref{tab:cross_arch_matrix}).
40	VLM-to-VLA fine-tuning is associated with substantial exit collapse across all tested conditions: TracVLA~\cite{zheng2024tracevla} $\Delta R^2 = 0.156$--$0.362$ across all four suites, and NORA~\cite{hung2025nora} $\Delta R^2 = 0.308$ (Spatial) and $0.455$ (Goal) (Figure~\ref{fig:r2_collapse}), all exceeding the $\Delta R^2 > 0.10$ threshold for substantive collapse (\S\ref{sec:cross_arch_analyses}).
41	Under VLA-to-VLA conditions, Op

_source line 11306 · 2026-05-23T19:25:50.048573+00:00_

57	
58	\paragraph{Cross-instruction probes.}
59	Cross-instruction probes validate the analysis framework on OpenVLA~\cite{kim2024_openvla}: as predicted by the causal mask, image\_mean instruction classification stays at chance ($10\%$) across all 32 layers in all conditions (full profile in \S\ref{sec:dense_routing}).
60	Trained readout achieves $100\%$ 10-way classification from L1 onward and maintains this through L31 (LOOCV-confirmed; \S\ref{sec:loocv}).
61	Untrained readout also reaches $100\%$ at L1 but decays to $82\%$ by L31; the readout token is thus the primary multimodal integration site, and instruction encoding remains stable across depth in the trained model.
62	All patterns reproduce with a linear probe (\S\ref{sec:linear_probe}).
63	
64	\paragraph{L1 attention gradient.}
65	At L1, three conditions form a gradient of instruction attention: randomly initialized networks allocate 0/32 instruction-dominant heads ($11.7\%$ text attention, $1.5\times$ text-to-image ratio), Llama-2 base~\cite{touvron2023_llama2} allocates 9/32 ($36.5\%$), and VLA-trained allocates 24/32 ($57.7\%$, $38.2\times$ text-to-image ratio; \S\ref{sec:attention_decomp}).
66	The 32-layer attention profile reveals qualitatively distinct organizations: Llama-2 base concentrates instruction attention at mid-depth (L9--L16: 15--22 heads), while VLA fine-tuning produces an entry-exit bookend pattern with L0--L1 at 21--24 heads, L31 at 18, and intermediate layers at 0--11 (\S\ref{sec:threshold_sensitivity}).
