760	Figure~\ref{fig:attention_appendix} shows the per-head L1 attention distribution at the readout token.
761	The three-level gradient (randomly initialized 0/32, Llama-2 base 9/32, VLA-trained 24/32 instruction-dominant heads) shows that language pretraining seeds partial instruction attention and VLA fine-tuning shifts it further toward instruction dominance.
762	
763	\begin{figure}[h]
764	\centering
765	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_l1_stacked_bar.pdf}
766	\caption{L1 attention from the readout token across three conditions, per head (sorted by instruction attention fraction). VLA-trained: 24/32 instruction-dominant heads ($57.7\%$ text attention). Llama-2 base: 9/32 heads ($36.5\%$). Untrained: 0/32 heads ($11.7\%$). See Table~\ref{tab:multilayer_attention} for quantitative comparison across layers.}
767	\label{fig:attention_appendix}
768	\end{figure}
769	
770	\subsubsection{Multi-Layer Attention Patterns}
771	\label{sec:multilayer_attention}
772	
773	Table~\ref{tab:multilayer_attention} shows a 4-layer subset of the full 32-layer attention profile.
774	The full profile (Figure~\ref{fig:multilayer_attention}) reveals three qualitatively distinct patterns: the randomly initialized model maintains 0 instruction-dominant heads at all 32 layers; Llama-2 base shows a mid-depth peak (L9--L16: 15--22 heads), consistent with typical autoregressive LM processing depth; VLA fine-tuning produces an entry-exit pattern (L0--L1: 21--24 heads, L31: 18 heads, intermediate layers 0--11).

_source line 11316 · 2026-05-23T19:25:53.564727+00:00_

998	\subsection{Cross-Architecture Action Decodability: $2 \times 2$ Design}
999	\label{sec:cross_arch_r2}
1000	
1001	Table~\ref{tab:2x2_r2} reports per-layer action prediction $R^2$ across two architectures (OpenVLA, TraceVLA) $\times$ two training conditions (VLA-trained, reference base LLM), providing a $2 \times 2$ factorial view of how per-layer action decodability differs between trained and reference conditions.
1002	
1003	\paragraph{Convergent reorganization from divergent starting points.}
1004	Untrained profiles differ qualitatively: OpenVLA (Llama-2) peaks at L0 ($R^2 = 0.629$) then collapses to near-zero at mid-layers (L20: $0.086$, std $= 0.311$), while Phi-3-V peaks at L6 ($0.578 \pm 0.018$) with gradual monotonic decline (late-layer std $= 0.068$).
1005	After VLA training, both architectures reach nearly identical mid-layer peaks—OpenVLA at L9 ($R^2 = 0.637 \pm 0.008$), TraceVLA at L11 ($0.634 \pm 0.009$)—with $\Delta = 0.003$ and overlapping CIs.
1006	Deep-layer variance drops sharply with training: OpenVLA std $0.277 \to 0.040$; Phi-3-V std $0.068 \to 0.011$.
1007	Peak $R^2$ values are comparable before and after training (reference peaks reach $91$--$99\%$ of trained), but peak locations shift deeper (L0$\to$L9; L6$\to$L11) and profile shapes transform from architecture-specific patterns to a shared mid-layer plateau.
1008	
1009	\paragraph{Summary.}
1010	Table~\ref{tab:2x2_r2_summary} condenses the key contrasts.
1011	The L0 values are identical between trained and reference within each architecture (shared frozen vision encoder + projector), confirming that training effects arise from LLM weight changes.
1012	The largest training gains appear at mid-to-deep layers (L15--L25 average: OpenVLA $+0.313$, Phi-3-V $+0.103$), precisely where reference profiles diverge most.

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_15/projects/vla-ssd/docs/paper/experiments.tex:15:OpenVLA provides a VLA-to-VLA transition because its reference checkpoint is an OXE-pretrained action model, whereas TracVLA and NORA provide VLM-to-VLA transitions from action-untrained VLM references.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_15/projects/vla-ssd/docs/paper/experiments.tex:21:\caption{\textbf{Cross-architecture evaluation matrix.} Rows denote VLA architectures and columns denote LIBERO task suites. Each cell reports the peak-to-exit action-probe drop, $\Delta R^2$. OpenVLA compares LIBERO-finetuned checkpoints against an OXE-pretrained action-capable VLA reference, yielding a VLA-to-VLA transition. TracVLA and NORA compare VLA-finetuned checkpoints against action-untrained VLM references, yielding VLM-to-VLA transitions. Gray cells indicate unavailable checkpoints.}
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_15/projects/vla-ssd/docs/paper/experiments.tex:136:NORA and action-untrained reference VLMs require representation-space metrics and are therefore reported separately.

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
5	\label{sec:setup}
6	
7	We apply all three analyses (\S\ref{sec:cross_arch_analyses}) to the $3 \times 4$ architecture-suite matrix described in \S\ref{sec:arch_data} (Table~\ref{tab:cross_arch_matrix}).
8	Of the 12 cells, 10 are filled; NORA Object and Long-Horizon lack available checkpoints.
9	For OpenVLA LIBERO-Goal, $N = 63{,}228$ observations; other cells are at comparable scale.
10	For trained models that produce action outputs, counterfactual sensitivity is measured via Cohen's $d$ on action MSE.
11	For reference VLMs (Phi-3-Vision, Qwen2.5-VL) that do not produce actions, sensitivity is measured via cosine distance on hidden representations; these metrics operate on different scales and are not directly comparable (\S\ref{sec:cf_sensitivity}).
12	OpenVLA's OXE-pretrained checkpoint produces actions, permitting same-metric (Cohen's $d$) comparison in that condition.
13	Bonferroni correction details appear in Appendix~\ref{sec:bonferroni_correction}.
14	Figure~\ref{fig:result_matrix} summarizes the $3 \times 4$ architecture-suite matrix.
15	OpenVLA provides a VLA-to-VLA transition because its reference checkpoint is an OXE-pretrained action model, whereas TracVLA and NORA provide VLM-to-VLA transitions from action-untrained VLM references.
16	The NORA Object and Long-Horizon cells are unavailable due to missing checkpoints.
17

OpenVLA compares LIBERO-finetuned checkpoints against an OXE-pretrained action-capable VLA reference, yielding a VLA-to-VLA transition. _source line 11358 · 2026-05-23T19:28:48.868906+00:00_

24	
25	Our analysis reveals three findings (Figure~\ref{fig:teaser}): (1)~VLM-to-VLA fine-tuning is associated with substantial $R^2$ exit collapse ($\Delta R^2 = 0.15$--$0.45$), whereas VLA-to-VLA fine-tuning and reference models show only small fluctuations ($\Delta R^2 < 0.10$), with an NLP control ($\Delta R^2 = 0.000$) consistent with transition-type specificity; (2)~all trained VLAs exhibit medium-to-large counterfactual sensitivity ($d = 0.45$--$1.52$); and (3)~L1 instruction attention reflects base-model priors, not VLA training (\S\ref{sec:r2_collapse}--\ref{sec:l1_priors}).

_source line 11398 · 2026-05-23T19:32:10.576470+00:00_

418	\subsubsection{Cross-Suite Counterfactual Replication}
419	\label{sec:spatial_counterfactual}
420	
421	Table~\ref{tab:counterfactual_spatial} reports counterfactual instruction analysis on LIBERO-Spatial (10 tasks).
422	The ordering differs from LIBERO-Goal: empty $>$ shuffled $>$ wrong $>$ paraphrased (vs.\ wrong $>$ empty $>$ shuffled $>$ paraphrased on Goal).
423	All effect sizes exceed $d = 0.8$.
424	Spatial instructions differ only in prepositions (e.g., ``on the plate'' vs.\ ``in the drawer''); the wrong-task $d = 0.885$ suggests that instruction-dominant heads encode fine-grained spatial relations, not merely task identity.
425	
426	\begin{table}[h]
427	\centering
428	\caption{Counterfactual instruction analysis on LIBERO-Spatial (10 tasks). Ordering: empty $>$ shuffled $>$ wrong $>$ paraphrased, differing from Goal. All $d > 0.8$.}
429	\label{tab:counterfactual_spatial}
430	\small
431	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
432	\toprule
433	Condition & Jaccard & Cohen's $d$ & CV \\
434	\midrule
435	Empty (no instruction) & 0.000 & 1.173 & 0.185 \\
436	Wrong (different task) & 0.174 & 0.885 & 0.093 \\
437	Shuffled (random order) & 1.000 & 0.979 & 0.166 \\
438	Paraphrased & 0.533 & 0.807 & 0.128 \\
439	\bottomrule
440	\end{tabular}
441	\end{table}
442	
443	\subsubsection{Cross-Instruction Probe on LIBERO-Spatial}
444	\label{sec:spatial_cross_instruction}
445	
446	Table~\ref{tab:spatial_cross_instruction} reports cross-instruction 10-way classification accuracy on LIBERO-Spatial.
447	The core pattern from LIBERO-Goal replicates: image\_mean remains at chance (${\sim}10\%$) across all layers and conditions, while the trained readout token saturates to $100\%$ by L4.

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Scope of claims.}
5	Our analyses are observational—we compare trained and reference checkpoints without intervening on training.
6	All findings are correlational; we cannot distinguish whether exit collapse is caused by action fine-tuning or by confounded factors (e.g., data distribution, optimization trajectory).
7	The cross-architecture design ($N = 2$ VLM-to-VLA transitions) strengthens external validity but remains insufficient for strong causal claims.
8	A claim-evidence summary appears in Figure~\ref{fig:claim_evidence}.
9	
10	\begin{figure}[t]
11	\centering
12	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_claim_evidence.pdf}
13	\caption{\textbf{Claim--evidence--scope summary.} Each main finding is paired with its supporting analysis and its main limitation. The figure distinguishes directly observed representational patterns from stronger causal interpretations that require interventional or training-dynamics evidence.}
14	\label{fig:claim_evidence}
15	\end{figure}
16	
17	\paragraph{Synthesis.}
18	VLA fine-tuning is associated with exit collapse and counterfactual sensitivity that concentrate at the readout token ($+0.069$ $R^2$ shift vs.\ $+0.015$ at image\_mean).
19	L1 instruction attention is inherited from base-model pretraining (Phi-3-Vision: 32/32, Qwen2.5-VL: 13--14/16 before training); VLA fine-tuning does not build instruction routing from scratch.
20	Exit collapse separates by transition type: substantial ($\Delta R^2 = 0.156$--$0.455$) at every VLM-to-VLA cell, but only small fluctuations ($\Delta R^2 = 0.033$--$0.086$) under VLA-to-VLA conditions, consistent with the OXE-pretrained baseline having already adapted at exit layers.
21	Mid-layer probes outperform exit-layer probes in architectures with pronounced collapse, suggesting potential early-exit efficiency gains ($\rho = 0.583$, $p = 0.10$; Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_discussion_appendix}).
22

_source line 11422 · 2026-05-23T19:33:35.535517+00:00_

1148	Counterfactual sensitivity is measured via Cohen's $d$ on action MSE for models that produce actions and via cosine distance on hidden representations for reference VLMs (\S\ref{sec:cf_sensitivity}).
1149	These metrics differ in scale and distributional properties; ratios between them are not meaningful.
1150	The OpenVLA Long-Horizon inversion (trained $d = 0.56$, reference $d = 1.05$) illustrates how same-metric comparisons can reverse the expected direction, cautioning against overinterpretation of any single cell.
1151	A standardized metric framework for cross-condition counterfactual comparison remains an open problem.
1152	
1153	\paragraph{Extended limitations.}
1154	Each architecture is analyzed at a single trained checkpoint, precluding characterization of how exit collapse or counterfactual sensitivity develop during training.
1155	All analyses are open-loop, consistent with prior representational studies~\cite{grant2026,haon2025}; closed-loop validation would provide complementary evidence about the behavioral relevance of the identified routing structures.
1156	The three-tier gradient has $n = 1$ at Tier~2, limiting its inferential power; populating this tier with additional general-purpose VLAs is a priority for future work.
1157	
1158

  "old_string": "\\subsubsection{Representation-Space Sensitivity in NORA and Reference VLMs}\n\\label{sec:cf_repr}\n\nNORA and action-untrained reference VLMs require representation-space metrics and are therefore reported separately.\nThese values should be interpreted as auxiliary evidence of representational sensitivity rather than direct action-space effect sizes.\nNORA Goal produces saturated exit-layer cosine distances of $1.0$ for all conditions; NORA Spatial yields a peak cosine distance of $0.275$.\nReference VLMs show weak sensitivity: the TracVLA reference (Phi-3-Vision) produces peak cosine distances of $0.15$--$0.19$; the NORA reference (Qwen2.5-VL) yields $0.081$ on Goal (see Appendix~\\ref{sec:cf_details_appendix} for metric discussion).",
  "new_string": "\\subsubsection{Representation-Space Sensitivity in NORA and Reference VLMs}\n\\label{sec:cf_repr}\n\nNORA and action-untrained reference VLMs lack action outputs; their sensitivity is measured via cosine distance on hidden representations, which operates on a different scale from Cohen's $d$ and should be interpreted as auxiliary evidence.\nNORA Goal produces saturated cosine distances ($1.0$); NORA Spatial peaks at $0.275$.\nReference VLMs show weak sensitivity ($\\leq 0.19$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:cf_details_appendix})."
}

_source line 11434 · 2026-05-23T19:34:40.589741+00:00_

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Scope of claims.}
5	Our analyses are observational—we compare trained and reference checkpoints without intervening on training.
6	All findings are correlational; we cannot distinguish whether exit collapse is caused by action fine-tuning or by confounded factors (e.g., data distribution, optimization trajectory).
7	The cross-architecture design ($N = 2$ VLM-to-VLA transitions) strengthens external validity but remains insufficient for strong causal claims.
8	A systematic claim--evidence--scope summary is provided in Figure~\ref{fig:claim_evidence} (Appendix).
9	
10	\paragraph{Synthesis.}
11	VLA fine-tuning is associated with exit collapse and counterfactual sensitivity that concentrate at the readout token ($+0.069$ $R^2$ shift vs.\ $+0.015$ at image\_mean).
12	L1 instruction attention is inherited from base-model pretraining (Phi-3-Vision: 32/32, Qwen2.5-VL: 13--14/16 before training); VLA fine-tuning does not build instruction routing from scratch.
13	Exit collapse separates by transition type: substantial ($\Delta R^2 = 0.156$--$0.455$) at every VLM-to-VLA cell, but only small fluctuations ($\Delta R^2 = 0.033$--$0.086$) under VLA-to-VLA conditions, consistent with the OXE-pretrained baseline having already adapted at exit layers.
14	Mid-layer probes outperform exit-layer probes in architectures with pronounced collapse, suggesting potential early-exit efficiency gains ($\rho = 0.583$, $p = 0.10$; Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_discussion_appendix}).
15	
16	\paragraph{Limitations.}
17	Cross-architecture evidence rests on $N = 2$ VLM-to-VLA transitions.
18	All experiments use LIBERO; benchmarks with richer language grounding are needed.
19	We analyze only causal-decoder VLAs; flow-matching~\cite{black2024_pi0} and diffusion~\cite{ghosh2024_octo} architectures may differ.
20	NORA lacks 2 of 4 suite checkpoints, and the NLP control covers only the Llama-2 backbone (extended discussion in App
…[truncated 700 chars]

720	\subsubsection{Cross-Architecture Probe and Attention Profile}
721	\label{app:finetuned_probe}

723	Table~\ref{tab:cross_arch_probe} reports cross-instruction probe accuracy and L1 instruction-dominant head counts across six conditions spanning two architectures.
724	The probe-attention dissociation at L1 is notable: TraceVLA training \emph{increases} instruction-dominant heads ($28 \to 32$) while \emph{decreasing} probe accuracy ($98.1\% \to 83.6\%$), consistent with representational reorganization for attention efficiency rather than linear separability.

14	
15	\begin{figure}[t]
16	\centering
17	\includegraphics[width=0.88\linewidth,height=2in,keepaspectratio]{figures/fig_teaser.pdf}
18	\vspace{-4pt}
19	\caption{\textbf{Overview of trained vs.\ reference representational differences across three VLA architectures.}
20	Top: per-layer action-probe $R^2$ profiles for VLM-to-VLA (TracVLA, NORA) and VLA-to-VLA (OpenVLA) transitions.
21	Bottom: L1 instruction-dominant head counts in trained and reference conditions.}
22	\label{fig:teaser}
23	\end{figure}
24	
25	Our analysis reveals three findings (Figure~\ref{fig:teaser}): (1)~VLM-to-VLA fine-tuning is associated with substantial $R^2$ exit collapse ($\Delta R^2 = 0.15$--$0.45$), whereas VLA-to-VLA fine-tuning and reference models show only small fluctuations ($\Delta R^2 < 0.10$), with an NLP control ($\Delta R^2 = 0.000$) consistent with transition-type specificity; (2)~all trained VLAs exhibit medium-to-large counterfactual sensitivity ($d = 0.45$--$1.52$); and (3)~L1 instruction attention reflects base-model priors, not VLA training (\S\ref{sec:r2_collapse}--\ref{sec:l1_priors}).
